About this tracker

This tool keeps an eye on Microsoft's Azure Kubernetes Service (AKS), AKS Application Network, Azure Arc-enabled Kubernetes, AKS Arc, Kubernetes Fleet Manager, Azure Linux, Azure Container Registry (ACR), and Azure Application Gateway for Containers (AGC) documentation. AKS everywhere! It also shows the last 5 AKS and App Net release notes.

It automatically scans for changes, then uses AI to summarize and highlight updates that are most likely to matter — such as new features, deprecations, and significant content revisions.

Minor edits (like typos, formatting tweaks, and other low-impact changes) are usually filtered out. Because the process is automated, some updates may be missed or summaries may not capture every nuance.

For complete accuracy, you can always follow the provided links to the original Microsoft documentation.

With this tracker, you can:

  • Quickly scan meaningful AKS, AKS Automatic, AKS Support, App Net, Arc K8s, AKS Arc, Azure Linux, ACR, AGC, and Fleet documentation changes from the past 7 days
  • Stay up to date with the latest AKS and App Net release notes without digging through every doc page
  • Search and browse CVE security data across AKS releases and VHD node images, powered by the AKS Vulnerability Data API (Public Preview)

Get the Latest AKS Docs - Every Week, in Your Inbox

AKS Releases

Latest 5 AKS and App Net release notes with AI-generated summaries, breaking changes, and Good to Know information.

updated: 30/10/2026, 12:13:16

Release 2026-10-25

AKS
View Release
📅 2026-10-27

The release on 2026-10-25 introduces automatic upgrades for unsupported Kubernetes versions and updates to node pools.

❌

Breaking Changes

  • AKS will automatically upgrade unsupported clusters after a grace period.
  • Default OS SKU for node pools changes to Ubuntu 26.04 with Kubernetes 1.38.
🔑

Key Features

  • Kubernetes 1.37 is now generally available as Long-Term Support.
  • Node Auto Provisioning (NAP) enhancements include new CRDs and features.
💡

Good to Know

  • Review upgrade channels to prepare for automated upgrades.
  • Community support for Kubernetes 1.34 ends by November 2026.

Release Notes 2026-09-25

AKS
View Release
📅 2026-10-01

The 2026-09-25 release introduces significant updates and deprecations. Key features include the general availability of identity bindings and preview support for Ubuntu 26.04.

❌

Breaking Changes

  • Windows Server 2022 is not supported in Kubernetes version 1.37 and above.
  • Flatcar Container Linux node images are removed, affecting scaling operations.
  • The annotation service.beta.kubernetes.io/azure-pip-tags no longer overrides public IP tags.
🔑

Key Features

  • Identity bindings are now generally available.
  • Ubuntu 26.04 is available in preview.
  • Windows Server OS SKU migration to 2025 is in preview.
💡

Good to Know

  • AKS will auto-migrate deprecated Availability Sets clusters after September 30, 2026.
  • AKS release order has been updated for Central US and East US.
  • Check AKS Release Tracker for detailed release timing.

Release 2026-09-04

AKS
View Release
📅 2026-09-09

This release introduces Kubernetes 1.37 and autoscaling for VM node pools. It also announces the retirement of Azure Linux with OS Guard.

❌

Breaking Changes

  • AKS will remove Azure Linux with OS Guard node images on March 10, 2027.
  • AKS no longer produces VHDs for Windows Server 2019 and version 23H2.
🔑

Key Features

  • Autoscaling for Virtual Machines node pools is now generally available.
  • Kubernetes patch versions 1.36.3, 1.35.7, and 1.34.10 are available.
  • On-Demand Monitor for node health checks is in preview.
💡

Good to Know

  • LocalDNS is enabled automatically for compatible cluster networking.
  • AKS rejects updates that remove IPv6 from dual-stack clusters.
  • Managed namespace creation rejects names starting with reserved prefixes.

AppNet 2026-08-19

App Net
View Release
📅 2026-08-31

AppNet is now in public preview with several updates and bug fixes.

❌

Breaking Changes

  • AppNet 1.0, 1.1, and 1.2 versions retired.
  • Moving AppNet resources across resource groups is now blocked.
🔑

Key Features

  • AppNet available in more Azure regions.
  • AppNet 1.5 updated to Istio 1.30.3.
💡

Good to Know

  • Cluster prerequisites validated at member-join time.
  • Fixed issues with member updates and version upgrades.

Release 2026-08-07

AKS
View Release
📅 2026-08-11

This release introduces several enhancements including Node Auto Provisioning and automatic availability zone placement.

🔑

Key Features

  • Node Auto Provisioning now supports restricted public network access
  • Automatic availability zone placement is enabled globally
  • Control-plane only upgrades to AKS Long Term Support are allowed
  • Node pool rollback feature is now generally available
  • Encryption in Transit for Azure File CSI driver is now available

Meaningful updates to AKS, AKS Automatic, AKS Support, App Net, Arc K8s, AKS Arc, Azure Linux, ACR, AGC, and Fleet docs from the last 7 days.

            AKS - App Routing

            Networking ✨ Update Detected: 2026-10-28 00:16 UTC
            Summary

            The documentation now includes a new section on multi-tenant clusters, highlighting that the managed NGINX Ingress controller does not provide isolation at the namespace level and should not be used as a security boundary in shared clusters. Users are informed about the implications of using Ingress objects across namespaces, including shared configurations and access to Secrets, along with recommended practices to mitigate risks in multi-tenant environments.

If you choose to inject your own sidecar into a managed proxy pod, Microsoft provides only best-effort support for any issues you encounter. +## Multi-tenant clusters++Important++The managed NGINX Ingress controller doesn't isolate tenants at the namespace level. Don't use it as a security boundary between tenants that share a cluster.++The managed NGINX Ingress controller is based on the upstream ingress-nginx project. The ingress-nginx project assumes that users who can create Ingress objects are cluster administrators. The project says not to use ingress-nginx in multi-tenant production clusters. For more information, see [Multi-tenant Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.github.io/ingress-nginx/faq/#multi-tenant-kubernetes) in the ingress-nginx FAQ.++The following behaviors cross namespace boundaries:++- One NGINX Ingress controller serves the Ingress objects of its Ingress class from all namespaces. The controller merges the rules of these Ingress objects into one NGINX configuration. For more information, see [How it works](https://kubernetes.github.io/ingress-nginx/how-it-works/#building-the-nginx-model).+- An Ingress in one namespace can add paths to a host that an Ingress in a different namespace uses. If two Ingress objects use the same host, the oldest Ingress object controls the TLS settings and server-level annotations for that host. If they also use the same path, the oldest Ingress object gets the traffic for that path.+- The add-on allows snippet annotations, except for the blocked values in Limitations. Snippet annotations add NGINX configuration to the shared controller.+- The NGINX Ingress controller can read Secrets in all namespaces. The add-on enables the [`allow-cross-namespace-resources`](https://kubernetes.github.io/ingress-nginx/user-guide/nginx-configuration/configmap/#allow-cross-namespace-resources) option. By using this option, some annotations can reference a Secret or ConfigMap in a different namespace.+- A user who can create an Ingress can use any Ingress class. A separate `NginxIngressController` for each tenant doesn't isolate tenants.++To decrease this risk, use these practices:++- Give permission to create or update Ingress objects only to users that you trust at the same level as cluster administrators.+- If tenants don't trust each other, use a separate cluster for each tenant. It's also responsible for updating the `Lease` objects that are related to the `Node` objects. +The following explains how the kubelet updates the node status and lease objects:+ - The kubelet updates the node `.status` when there's a change in status or if there hasn't been an update for a configured interval. The default interval for `.status` updates to nodes is five minutes, which is much longer than the 40-second default time-out for unreachable nodes. - The kubelet creates and then updates its `Lease` object every 10 seconds (the default update interval). `Lease` updates occur independently from updates to the node `.status`.                                         @@ -12,6 +12,81 @@ These updates cover security information related to the following AKS components - Azure Kubernetes Service Node Image (AKS Node Image) - Azure Kubernetes Service add-ons (AKS add-ons) +## AKS-2026-0006 OpenSSL end-of-life scanner findings in AKS-managed add-on images++**Published date**: October 21, 2026++### Description++Some vulnerability scanners report OpenSSL 3.3 as end of life (EOL) in AKS-managed add-on container images. This article explains why an upstream EOL warning alone doesn't establish that the Azure Linux package lacks security maintenance.++OpenSSL 3.3 reached [upstream EOL on April 9, 2026](https://openssl-library.org/roadmap/index.html). Azure Linux 3.0 continues to apply security fixes to its OpenSSL package through backports. A backport applies a fix without changing the upstream version number.++The [Azure Linux OpenSSL package changelog](https://github.com/microsoft/azurelinux/blob/59dd31f473b6eca8d05410731653177444d6a416/SPECS/openssl/openssl.spec#L387-L401) documents security fixes after that date:++| Azure Linux package revision | Changelog date | Example security fix |+| --- | --- | --- |+| `3.3.7-4.azl3` | July 16, 2026 | [CVE-2026-45447 patch](https://github.com/microsoft/azurelinux/blob/59dd31f473b6eca8d05410731653177444d6a416/SPECS/openssl/CVE-2026-45447.patch) |+| `3.3.7-6.azl3` | August 26, 2026 | [CVE-2026-54874 patch](https://github.com/microsoft/azurelinux/blob/59dd31f473b6eca8d05410731653177444d6a416/SPECS/openssl/CVE-2026-54874.patch) |++These examples show backporting, not a minimum package version or a claim that every image contains every fix.++Important++This article doesn't classify all OpenSSL findings as false positives. An upstream EOL warning and a missing security patch are different findings. Assess each reported CVE against the exact Azure Linux package revision.++### Why scanners report these findings++The `openssl version` command reports the upstream version, such as `3.3.7`, without the Azure Linux package revision, such as `-4.azl3`. The image's RPM package metadata and software bill of materials (SBOM) contain the complete package version.++A scanner that evaluates only the upstream version can miss Microsoft's backported fixes. The upstream EOL date is correct, but an inference that Microsoft no longer services the Azure Linux package is incorrect.++Some scanners also report CVEs without accounting for distribution-specific fixes. The [Azure Linux 3.0 vulnerability feed](https://github.com/microsoft/AzureLinuxVulnerabilityData/blob/main/azurelinux-3.0-oval.xml) provides Open Vulnerability and Assessment Language (OVAL) definitions for evaluating CVEs against package revisions. Distribution-aware CVE matching doesn't remove an EOL-only warning that has no associated CVE.++The container's base operating system is separate from the node's operating system. An add-on running on an Ubuntu node can use an Azure Linux base image. A node image upgrade doesn't change the packages inside that container image.++### Evidence from a published image++For the `linux/amd64` image `mcr.microsoft.com/oss/v2/kubernetes/coredns:v1.14.3-20`, the [digest-pinned SPDX SBOM](https://mcr.microsoft.com/v2/oss/v2/kubernetes/coredns/blobs/sha256:6f5dde31f4349cfbf4f532dc9b8ab090409047e6beb74c6cf8a5542801d65481) records:++| Package | Complete package version |+| --- | --- |+| `azurelinux-release` | `3.0-49.azl3` |+| `openssl` | `3.3.7-4.azl3` |+| `openssl-libs` | `3.3.7-4.azl3` |++The public [image manifest](https://mcr.microsoft.com/v2/oss/v2/kubernetes/coredns/manifests/sha256:6903c44b9016c274048ffc4b1b3691fb6ac6ccd82f2d2e19032142f80cea9339) and [attestation manifest](https://mcr.microsoft.com/v2/oss/v2/kubernetes/coredns/manifests/sha256:1f52d1dfa4fab42f5a7ab597ebb38782a85f7977193a7e6a15fcb5b53f307d2e) link that image to this SBOM. These links use immutable digests rather than a moving tag.++This historical image illustrates package identification, not a recommendation to deploy it or a statement that it has no vulnerabilities. Check your deployed digest separately.++### References++- [OpenSSL release roadmap and upstream support dates](https://openssl-library.org/roadmap/index.html).+- [Azure Linux OpenSSL package and backport changelog](https://github.com/microsoft/azurelinux/blob/59dd31f473b6eca8d05410731653177444d6a416/SPECS/openssl/openssl.spec#L387-L401).+- [Azure Linux 3.0 vulnerability data and OVAL definitions](https://github.com/microsoft/AzureLinuxVulnerabilityData/blob/main/azurelinux-3.0-oval.xml).+- [Vulnerability management for AKS](../concepts-vulnerability-management).++### Affected components++# [AKS add-ons](#tab/aks-addons)+**Affected versions**++- AKS-managed add-on images based on Azure Linux 3.0 that contain OpenSSL 3.3 packages can trigger these findings.+- Examples include CoreDNS, metrics-server, and Azure Disk and Azure Files CSI components. The finding depends on the exact image and the scanner's detection rules, not a single Kubernetes version.+- This bulletin doesn't cover OpenSSL packages in node images or customer-managed containers.++**Resolutions**++No manual OpenSSL replacement is needed for a verified EOL-only finding in the scope of this bulletin. AKS manages updates to its add-on images. A newer add-on image can contain security fixes and still report the same upstream OpenSSL version.++- **Verify the package.** Record the image repository, tag, and digest from the scanner report. Confirm Azure Linux 3.0 and the complete `openssl` or `openssl-libs` package revision from that image's SBOM or RPM metadata. Don't use `openssl version` alone to assess the package's patch status.+- **Separate EOL warnings from CVEs.** For a specific CVE, compare the installed package revision with Azure Linux's published fix information. Ask your scanner vendor to use distribution-aware matching and current vulnerability data.+- **Handle verified EOL-only findings through your security policy.** Consider a time-bound exception for the exact image digest and EOL finding. Attach the package version, the published backport evidence, and a review date. Don't extend the exception to unresolved CVEs.+- **Keep managed components intact.** Don't replace or modify AKS-managed add-on images to change the reported OpenSSL version. Continue normal cluster and node image updates for other security fixes.+- **Escalate findings outside this scope.** Contact Azure support if your report identifies a missing security patch or differs from this bulletin's scope. Include the image digest, full package version, finding identifier, and scanner output.++---+ ## AKS-2026-0005 Increased Ubuntu 22.04 Linux kernel CVE findings from Canonical reclassifications  **Published Date**: July 7, 2026

For public clusters, the nodes try to communicate with the Microsoft Container Registry (MCR) endpoint (`mcr.microsoft.com`) on port 443.+During node provisioning, the Custom Script Extension (CSE) runs a connectivity check against the Microsoft Container Registry (MCR) endpoint by running `curl -v --insecure --proxy-insecure https://mcr.microsoft.com/v2/`. The check retries several times. The sample uses one head pod and one worker pod.++Set the sample resource names and Ray image:++```bash+RAY_NAMESPACE="kuberay-sample"+RAY_CLUSTER_NAME="sample-raycluster"+RAY_JOB_NAME="sample-rayjob"+RAY_VERSION="2.58.0"+RAY_IMAGE="mcr.microsoft.com/aks/ai-runtime/ray@sha256:e9a329eef15e5a13d50c3186c2b8036cd3728c6e218da860de23e8494b99ddd3"+```++Create a namespace and deploy the `RayCluster`:++```bash+kubectl create namespace "$RAY_NAMESPACE"++kubectl apply -f - <<YAML+apiVersion: ray.io/v1+kind: RayCluster+metadata:+  name: ${RAY_CLUSTER_NAME}+  namespace: ${RAY_NAMESPACE}+spec:+  rayVersion: "${RAY_VERSION}"+  headGroupSpec:+    rayStartParams:+      num-cpus: "0"+      dashboard-host: "0.0.0.0"+    template:+      spec:+        containers:+        - name: ray-head+          image: ${RAY_IMAGE}+          resources:+            requests:+              cpu: 500m+              memory: 2Gi+            limits:+              cpu: "1"+              memory: 4Gi+  workerGroupSpecs:+  - groupName: cpu-workers+    replicas: 1+    minReplicas: 1+    maxReplicas: 1+    rayStartParams:+      num-cpus: "1"+    template:+      spec:+        containers:+        - name: ray-worker+          image: ${RAY_IMAGE}+          resources:+            requests:+              cpu: 500m+              memory: 1Gi+            limits:+              cpu: "1"+              memory: 2Gi+YAML+```++Wait for the `RayCluster` to report `ready`, and then confirm that the head and worker pods are running:++```bash+kubectl wait \+  --for=jsonpath='{.status.state}'=ready \+  raycluster/"$RAY_CLUSTER_NAME" \+  --namespace "$RAY_NAMESPACE" \+  --timeout=10m++kubectl get raycluster,pods --namespace "$RAY_NAMESPACE"+```++Submit an HTTP-mode `RayJob` to the existing cluster. The job runs four remote tasks on the worker and prints a success marker after validating their results:++```bash+kubectl apply -f - <<YAML+apiVersion: ray.io/v1+kind: RayJob+metadata:+  name: ${RAY_JOB_NAME}+  namespace: ${RAY_NAMESPACE}+spec:+  jobId: ${RAY_JOB_NAME}+  submissionMode: HTTPMode+  clusterSelector:+    ray.io/cluster: ${RAY_CLUSTER_NAME}+  entrypoint: >-+    python3 -c "import ray; ray.init(address='auto'); square=ray.remote(lambda x: x*x);+    values=ray.get([square.remote(i) for i in range(4)]);+    assert values == [0, 1, 4, 9], values;+    print('KUBERAY_SAMPLE_OK', values, flush=True)"+  shutdownAfterJobFinishes: false+YAML+```++Wait for the job to succeed:++```bash+kubectl wait \+  --for=jsonpath='{.status.jobStatus}'=SUCCEEDED \+  rayjob/"$RAY_JOB_NAME" \+  --namespace "$RAY_NAMESPACE" \+  --timeout=10m+```++Read the Ray driver log from the head pod:++```bash+HEAD_POD="$(kubectl get pods \+  --namespace "$RAY_NAMESPACE" \+  --selector="ray.io/cluster=$RAY_CLUSTER_NAME,ray.io/node-type=head" \+  --output jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}')"++kubectl exec \+  --namespace "$RAY_NAMESPACE" \+  "$HEAD_POD" \+  --container ray-head \+  -- sh -c 'grep -h "KUBERAY_SAMPLE_OK" /tmp/ray/session_latest/logs/job-driver-*.log'+```++The expected output is:++```output+KUBERAY_SAMPLE_OK [0, 1, 4, 9]+```++This output confirms that the operator created the Ray cluster, submitted the job, and ran tasks on the worker. Delete the sample resources when you're finished:++```bash+kubectl delete namespace "$RAY_NAMESPACE"+```+ ## Update the managed KubeRay extension  Use the `az k8s-extension extension-types list-versions-by-cluster` command in Install the managed KubeRay extension to check which versions are available for your cluster.

                                                                                                                    AKS - Istio Meshconfig

                                                                                                                    Networking ✨ Update Detected: 2026-10-28 00:16 UTC
                                                                                                                    Summary

                                                                                                                    The documentation now specifies that the parameters `defaultTrafficPolicy`, `serviceEntryVisibility`, `zoneAwareLbSetting`, and `connectionSettings` are configurable starting with asm-1-31. This allows users to customize these settings for the Istio-based service mesh add-on in Azure Kubernetes Service, enhancing their control over traffic management and service visibility.
